Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more. 

Um... I suppose what brings me in is... well, I've been having some thoughts, and I feel a bit ashamed even saying it, to be honest. I don't want you to think poorly of me, or think I'm ungrateful for my family. But... I just keep thinking that my wife and the kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I know that's a terrible thing to say, isn't it? 

And I guess, lately, I’ve been saying out loud that I really don’t need to see another winter. I don't mean to sound so gloomy or be a bother to anyone... I hope it's alright that I'm sharing this with you?

Oh, well... to be perfectly honest, and I hate to admit this because I don't want you to think I've just gotten lazy in my old age... but no, I haven't been. 

I used to really look forward to so many things, you know? Playing bridge with friends, tending to the garden, helping out with the church cooking... even going to the Cubs games. But lately... I don't know what's wrong with me. I mostly just sit in my chair. I tell myself I should get up and do things, I really do try, but I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore. 

I know I should be keeping active, and I feel terrible for just sitting around the house all day. Is that... is that normal for someone my age, Doctor? I really hope I'm not just giving up.

Oh, my... well, I suppose I might be a little slower these days. I really hope it's just my age catching up with me, and I certainly don't want to make a fuss about it.

I hope it's alright that I'm sharing this with you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's been quite a while now. I hope I haven't waited too long to come in, I really didn't want to make a fuss about it. \n\nIt's just... I think it started right around the time my bowling partner passed away. That was about a year and a month ago. I try my best to shake it off, I really do, because I know I should be stronger than this and I don't want to drag everyone down with me. But it's just been lingering since then. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it alright.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you used to like doing, such as bowling, as much as you did before your bowling partner passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... to be perfectly honest, and I hate to admit this because I don't want you to think I've just gotten lazy in my old age... but no, I haven't been. \n\nI used to really look forward to so many things, you know? Playing bridge with friends, tending to the garden, helping out with the church cooking... even going to the Cubs games. But lately... I don't know what's wrong with me. I mostly just sit in my chair. I tell myself I should get up and do things, I really do try, but I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore. \n\nI know I should be keeping active, and I feel terrible for just sitting around the house all day. Is that... is that normal for someone my age, Doctor? I really hope I'm not just giving up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling pleasure or enjoying things in general, even if it's not the activities you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I wish I could give you a better answer, Doctor, I really do. I know I ought to be finding joy in the little things, especially at my age. Even when I know I should be happy, I just can't seem to feel it. I feel terrible admitting that to you, because it sounds so terribly ungrateful. I really don't want to be that kind of person.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would end your life if you decided to act on those thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I really don't want to alarm you, Doctor. I wouldn't want to put them through that. \n\nI don't have a specific plan with a weapon or anything like that. I don't want you to think poorly of me, or think I'm ungrateful for my family. But... I just keep thinking that my wife and the kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I know that's a terrible thing to say, isn't it? \n\nAnd I guess, lately, I’ve been saying out loud that I really don’t need to see another winter. I don't mean to sound so gloomy or be a bother to anyone... I hope it's alright that I'm sharing this with you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's been quite a while now. I hope I haven't waited too long to come in, I really didn't want to make a fuss about it. \n\nIt's just... I think it started right around the time my bowling partner passed away. That was about a year and a month ago. I try my best to shake it off, I really do, because I know I should be stronger than this and I don't want to drag everyone down with me. But it's just been lingering since then. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it alright.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you used to like doing, such as bowling, as much as you did before your bowling partner passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... to be perfectly honest, and I hate to admit this because I don't want you to think I've just gotten lazy in my old age... but no, I haven't been. \n\nI used to really look forward to so many things, you know? Playing bridge with friends, tending to the garden, helping out with the church cooking... even going to the Cubs games. But lately... I don't know what's wrong with me. I mostly just sit in my chair. I tell myself I should get up and do things, I really do try, but I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore. \n\nI know I should be keeping active, and I feel terrible for just sitting around the house all day. Is that... is that normal for someone my age, Doctor? I really hope I'm not just giving up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling pleasure or enjoying things in general, even if it's not the activities you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I wish I could give you a better answer, Doctor, I really do. I know I ought to be finding joy in the little things, especially at my age. I have a warm house, my wife is so good to me... but, to be completely honest with you, I just haven't been feeling that spark of pleasure at all. \n\nEverything just feels sort of... empty, I suppose. Even when I know I should be happy, I just can't seem to feel it. I feel terrible admitting that to you, because it sounds so terribly ungrateful. I really am trying to look on the bright side, I promise I am, it's just... it's just not there right now. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself? I really don't want to be that kind of person.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would end your life if you decided to act on those thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I really don't want to alarm you, Doctor. And I'd hate for you to think I'm going to do something drastic or... or make a terrible mess for my family. I wouldn't want to put them through that. \n\nI don't have a specific plan with a weapon or anything like that. It's more just... well, to be honest, what actually brings me in today is that I just keep thinking my wife and the kids would be so much better off if I didn't wake up at all. I haven't figured out exactly how to make that happen... but lately, I've just been saying that I don't need to see another winter. I just kind of hope I drift off and it happens on its own, I suppose. \n\nI'm so sorry if that sounds awful to hear. I feel terribly guilty even saying it out loud. I really don't mean to be a bother with all this dark talk... am I making sense, Doctor? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I know we just talked about this, and I really, really wish I could give you a better answer this time around. I hate being so repetitive, and I certainly don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not trying to get better.\n\nBut... no. I can't really think of a single thing right now. Not even reading the morning paper or having a cup of coffee. It all just feels like going through the motions. I know I should be trying harder to find something, anything, to enjoy. I really don't mean to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I haven't really noticed anything like that, Doctor. I know I should probably be keeping better track of these things for you. My wife makes sure I have three meals a day, she's wonderful about that, so I'm sure my weight is just fine. I haven't been stepping on the scale or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. \n\nI hope that's an alright answer? I'd hate for you to think I'm neglecting my health on top of everything else. I really am trying to eat what she puts in front of me, even if I don't have much of an appetite. I'm doing my best to be a good patient for you, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I suppose I might be a little slower these days. I really hope it's just my age catching up with me, and I certainly don't want to make a fuss about it. \n\nBut, well, my wife... she's so patient with me, bless her heart... she did mention the other day that I move like I'm walking through deep snow. Even just trying to get dressed in the morning or walking across the kitchen to get a glass of water... it just takes me so very long now. Sometimes I get so worn out just trying to do those little things that I just give up and sit back down before I've really accomplished anything at all. \n\nI feel terrible being such a burden to her, having her wait on me like that. I know I should be moving faster and doing more around the house. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing as well as I should be, Doctor. Is it alright that I'm struggling with this? I really am trying my best to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I don't think I'm sleeping more, really. I mean, I spend a lot of time in my armchair, but I wouldn't call it resting, exactly. \n\nI do hope I'm not coming across as lazy, Doctor. I know I should be up and about, being productive, but sometimes I just sit there for hours. I'm not really sleeping, just... sitting. My wife checks on me, and I always make sure to tell her I'm just taking a little breather. I don't want her to worry that I'm just wasting the day away. \n\nIs that the sort of thing you were asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I'm not trying to hide anything, I just... I don't think I'm actually sleeping more than a man my age ought to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Oh... oh, dear. I... well, I suppose I do feel terrible about it. I really shouldn't be thinking such things, should I? It's just so ungrateful of me, especially when my wife tries so hard to take care of me.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... no, not my normal pace at all, I'm afraid. Like I was just mentioning a moment ago... and I'm so sorry to repeat myself, Doctor, I hope it's alright that I'm saying it again. But yes, my wife says it's like I'm walking through deep snow. Everything is just terribly slow now.

I'd hate for you to think I'm neglecting my health on top of everything else. I really am trying to eat what she puts in front of me, even if I don't have much of an appetite. I'm doing my best to be a good patient for you, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I suppose I might be a little slower these days. I really hope it's just my age catching up with me, and I certainly don't want to make a fuss about it. \n\nBut, well, my wife... she's so patient with me, bless her heart... she did mention the other day that I move like I'm walking through deep snow. Even just trying to get dressed in the morning or walking across the kitchen to get a glass of water... it just takes me so very long now. Sometimes I get so worn out just trying to do those little things that I just give up and sit back down before I've really accomplished anything at all. \n\nI feel terrible being such a burden to her, having her wait on me like that. I know I should be moving faster and doing more around the house. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing as well as I should be, Doctor. Is it alright that I'm struggling with this? I really am trying my best to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I don't think I'm sleeping more, really. My wife checks on me, and I always make sure to tell her I'm just taking a little breather. I don't want her to worry that I'm just wasting the day away. \n\nIs that the sort of thing you were asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I don't think I'm actually sleeping more than a man my age ought to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you couldn't enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I suppose it's been a little while now. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more. \n\nUm... I suppose what brings me in is... well, I've been having some thoughts, and I feel a bit ashamed even saying it, to be honest. I don't want you to think poorly of me, or think I'm ungrateful for my family. But... I just keep thinking that my wife and the kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I know that's a terrible thing to say, isn't it? \n\nAnd I guess, lately, I’ve been saying out loud that I really don’t need to see another winter. I don't mean to sound so gloomy or be a bother to anyone... I hope it's alright that I'm sharing this with you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's been quite a while now. I hope I haven't waited too long to come in, I really didn't want to make a fuss about it. \n\nIt's just... I think it started right around the time my bowling partner passed away. That was about a year and a month ago. I try my best to shake it off, I really do, because I know I should be stronger than this and I don't want to drag everyone down with me. But it's just been lingering since then. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it alright.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you used to like doing, such as bowling, as much as you did before your bowling partner passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... to be perfectly honest, and I hate to admit this because I don't want you to think I've just gotten lazy in my old age... but no, I haven't been. \n\nI used to really look forward to so many things, you know? Playing bridge with friends, tending to the garden, helping out with the church cooking... even going to the Cubs games. But lately... I don't know what's wrong with me. I mostly just sit in my chair. I tell myself I should get up and do things, I really do try, but I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore. \n\nI know I should be keeping active, and I feel terrible for just sitting around the house all day. Is that... is that normal for someone my age, Doctor? I really hope I'm not just giving up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling pleasure or enjoying things in general, even if it's not the activities you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I wish I could give you a better answer, Doctor, I really do. I know I ought to be finding joy in the little things, especially at my age. I have a warm house, my wife is so good to me... but, to be completely honest with you, I just haven't been feeling that spark of pleasure at all. \n\nEverything just feels sort of... empty, I suppose. Even when I know I should be happy, I just can't seem to feel it. I feel terrible admitting that to you, because it sounds so terribly ungrateful. I really am trying to look on the bright side, I promise I am, it's just... it's just not there right now. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself? I really don't want to be that kind of person.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would end your life if you decided to act on those thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I really don't want to alarm you, Doctor. And I'd hate for you to think I'm going to do something drastic or... or make a terrible mess for my family. I wouldn't want to put them through that. \n\nI don't have a specific plan with a weapon or anything like that. It's more just... well, to be honest, what actually brings me in today is that I just keep thinking my wife and the kids would be so much better off if I didn't wake up at all. I haven't figured out exactly how to make that happen... but lately, I've just been saying that I don't need to see another winter. I just kind of hope I drift off and it happens on its own, I suppose. \n\nI'm so sorry if that sounds awful to hear. I feel terribly guilty even saying it out loud. I really don't mean to be a bother with all this dark talk... am I making sense, Doctor? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I know we just talked about this, and I really, really wish I could give you a better answer this time around. I hate being so repetitive, and I certainly don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not trying to get better.\n\nBut... no. I can't really think of a single thing right now. Not even reading the morning paper or having a cup of coffee. It all just feels like going through the motions. I know I should be trying harder to find something, anything, to enjoy. I feel awful that I can't give you a positive answer, Doctor. Am I being a terribly difficult patient today? I really don't mean to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I haven't really noticed anything like that, Doctor. I know I should probably be keeping better track of these things for you. My wife makes sure I have three meals a day, she's wonderful about that, so I'm sure my weight is just fine. I haven't been stepping on the scale or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. \n\nI hope that's an alright answer? I'd hate for you to think I'm neglecting my health on top of everything else. I really am trying to eat what she puts in front of me, even if I don't have much of an appetite. I'm doing my best to be a good patient for you, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I suppose I might be a little slower these days. I really hope it's just my age catching up with me, and I certainly don't want to make a fuss about it. \n\nBut, well, my wife... she's so patient with me, bless her heart... she did mention the other day that I move like I'm walking through deep snow. Even just trying to get dressed in the morning or walking across the kitchen to get a glass of water... it just takes me so very long now. Sometimes I get so worn out just trying to do those little things that I just give up and sit back down before I've really accomplished anything at all. \n\nI feel terrible being such a burden to her, having her wait on me like that. I know I should be moving faster and doing more around the house. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing as well as I should be, Doctor. Is it alright that I'm struggling with this? I really am trying my best to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I don't think I'm sleeping more, really. I mean, I spend a lot of time in my armchair, but I wouldn't call it resting, exactly. \n\nI do hope I'm not coming across as lazy, Doctor. I know I should be up and about, being productive, but sometimes I just sit there for hours. I'm not really sleeping, just... sitting. My wife checks on me, and I always make sure to tell her I'm just taking a little breather. I don't want her to worry that I'm just wasting the day away. \n\nIs that the sort of thing you were asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I'm not trying to hide anything, I just... I don't think I'm actually sleeping more than a man my age ought to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you couldn't enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I suppose it's been a little while now. I really hope you don't think I'm just being ungrateful for the life I have. I know I have so much to be thankful for, especially with my wife taking such good care of me. \n\nBut, um... if I have to put a timeline on it... I guess it started after my bowling partner passed away. That was about... oh, a year and a month ago, I think? We used to go every Tuesday, and it was such a nice time. After he died, I just... I couldn't bring myself to go back. And then it seemed like other things just sort of lost their shine, too. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I should be trying harder to find joy in things, especially for my wife's sake. I don't want to be a gloomy presence in the house. I'm trying to keep my spirits up, I really am. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for, Doctor? I want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I'm actually quite still most of the day. Like I mentioned, I mostly just sit quietly in my chair. \n\nI try very hard not to be a bother, you know? Pacing around or fidgeting... well, I imagine that would make my poor wife terribly nervous, and she already does so much for me. I certainly wouldn't want to add to her worries by acting all restless and agitated. I just try to stay put and stay out of her way.\n\nI hope it's alright that I'm more on the quiet side. Is it a good sign that I'm not fidgeting, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way and giving you the answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about having these thoughts of not waking up?\n\nPatient: Oh... oh, dear. I... well, I suppose I do feel terrible about it. I really shouldn't be thinking such things, should I? It's just so ungrateful of me, especially when my wife tries so hard to take care of me. \n\nBut... I just sit there in my chair, and my mind just spins. I keep going over every little thing I did wrong with my bowling partner, and... and with my family over the years. All the mistakes I've made. It just feels like I'm no good to anybody anymore. I'm just a burden. Sometimes I think... well, I feel like I deserve whatever punishment is coming my way for being such a failure to them. \n\nI'm so sorry, Doctor. I know that sounds awful, and I really don't want you to think poorly of me. I know I should be stronger than this. Am I making sense? I just want to be honest with you, but I hope I'm not making things too difficult.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose I do. I mean, I really hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I know it's not very becoming for a man my age to talk like this. \n\nBut... like I said earlier, I just sit there and my mind goes round and round, thinking about all the things I did wrong with my bowling partner, and all the ways I let my family down over the years. I just feel like I'm completely useless now... like I'm no good to anybody at all. And... well, I feel like I deserve whatever punishment is coming my way for all my mistakes. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I'm probably repeating myself. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly. Is this the kind of information you need from me, Doctor? I really am trying my best to be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more quickly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, not quickly at all. Actually, it's quite the opposite, I'm afraid. I hope that's not a bad thing. \n\nMy wife... well, she tells me I move like I'm walking through deep snow these days. Everything just feels so heavy and slow. Even simple things, like getting dressed in the morning or just crossing the kitchen to get a glass of water... it takes me such a long time now. Sometimes I just... I just give up before I've really done much of anything because it's so exhausting. \n\nI feel terrible making her wait on me when I'm moving so slowly. I really try to hurry for her, I do, but my body just won't cooperate. Is it normal to be this slow, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things too difficult for everyone by being this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving at a normal pace compared to how you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, not my normal pace at all, I'm afraid. Like I was just mentioning a moment ago... and I'm so sorry to repeat myself, Doctor, I hope it's alright that I'm saying it again. But yes, my wife says it's like I'm walking through deep snow. Everything is just terribly slow now. \n\nEven just trying to get dressed in the morning, or... or crossing the kitchen to get somewhere... it takes me so long that I usually just give up before I've done much of anything. I really hate to admit that to you. I know I shouldn't give up so easily, and I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or difficult. I want to do better, I really do. Is that the sort of detail you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or have an inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, not restless at all. I hope you don't mind me bringing this up again, Doctor, but... well, like I was saying, it's really quite the opposite for me. \n\nI don't have any trouble sitting still... in fact, sitting still is about all I seem to do these days. My wife, she... she tells me I move like I'm trudging through deep snow. Everything is just so incredibly heavy and slow. Even something simple, like getting dressed or just trying to cross the kitchen... it takes me such a long time that I usually just end up giving up before I've really accomplished anything. \n\nI know I shouldn't just give up like that, and I'm so sorry if that sounds like I'm not trying. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around on purpose. I want to be helpful and active, I really do. Does that answer your question alright, Doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you felt like you couldn't enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's been quite a while now. I hope I don't sound like I'm just complaining, Doctor. I know I should just snap out of it and be grateful for the time I have left... I really do try to be positive. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest, this all started right around the time my bowling partner passed away. That was... oh, about a year and a month ago now. I just haven't been able to find the joy in much of anything since then. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that out loud, like I'm letting everyone down by not moving on properly. I really should be doing better by now, shouldn't I? I hope that's the timeline you were looking for... am I explaining this alright for your notes? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh dear... I am so sorry, Doctor. Did I... did I not explain that clearly enough just a moment ago? I feel just awful making you ask me the exact same thing again. I must be doing a poor job of answering your questions today, and I really do want to be helpful.
